I don’t think it’s exactly because I want to sit and think about it, but I also wouldn’t say it feels like I can’t stop myself. It just comes up when something reminds me, and then I feel bad about it for a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about the fundraiser when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes, but not in a way that feels like it’s stuck in my head. It’ll pop up when I see something related to it, and I wish I wasn’t thinking about it, but then it usually passes after a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts about the fundraiser come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, it can come into my mind when I wasn’t planning to think about it, but usually there’s some reminder. I don’t know if I’d say it just comes out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having thoughts about the fundraiser that you can't control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say I can’t control them. They come up, and I don’t like it when they do, but I can usually shift my mind to something else after a little while.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about the fundraiser even when you're trying to fall asleep or relax?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been messing with my sleep some. For a while I was only sleeping a few hours and then waking up like I was ready to start sewing or making phone calls. Lately it’s more like I lie there worrying, or I sleep late and still feel worn out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts about the fundraiser are forcing their way into your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it forcing its way in. It pops up sometimes, especially if I’m already stressed, but it doesn’t feel like something taking over my mind exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having intrusive thoughts about the fundraiser that are unwanted and disturbing?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them intrusive exactly. They’re unwanted, sure, and upsetting sometimes, but it feels more like worry that keeps coming back than some disturbing thought I can’t get rid of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when you're not thinking about the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I do feel on edge a lot lately, but I haven’t paid attention to whether it’s there when the fundraiser isn’t on my mind. I guess I’d have to think about that more.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.0, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I think I took on way too much with this historical society fundraiser. I was acting like I could run the whole thing myself—starting quilt designs, calling volunteers, making schedules, promising deadlines—and now it’s pretty clear I couldn’t possibly keep up with all of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying about this fundraiser even when there wasn't anything you could do about it at the moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’d think about it when something reminded me, but I don’t know that I was just worrying nonstop when there was nothing I could do. It’s more that the whole thing got bigger than I could handle.\n\nDoctor: Have you been very hard on yourself about not being able to keep up with the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ve been pretty hard on myself. I keep going back over every late order, and every sharp thing I said to the volunteers, and I just think... I let the church down, and the historical society, and really the whole town.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the same thoughts or urges related to the fundraiser, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it repeated urges or anything like that. I do think about it, especially when I’m reminded of something I didn’t finish, but I haven’t really noticed it taking over my mind when I’m trying to focus on something else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about the fundraiser because you want to, or because you can't stop yourself from thinking about it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think it’s exactly because I want to sit and think about it, but I also wouldn’t say it feels like I can’t stop myself. It just comes up when something reminds me, and then I feel bad about it for a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about the fundraiser when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes, but not in a way that feels like it’s stuck in my head. It’ll pop up when I see something related to it, and I wish I wasn’t thinking about it, but then it usually passes after a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts about the fundraiser come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, it can come into my mind when I wasn’t planning to think about it, but usually there’s some reminder. I don’t know if I’d say it just comes out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having thoughts about the fundraiser that you can't control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say I can’t control them. They come up, and I don’t like it when they do, but I can usually shift my mind to something else after a little while.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about the fundraiser even when you're trying to fall asleep or relax?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been messing with my sleep some. For a while I was only sleeping a few hours and then waking up like I was ready to start sewing or making phone calls. Lately it’s more like I lie there worrying, or I sleep late and still feel worn out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts about the fundraiser are forcing their way into your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it forcing its way in. It pops up sometimes, especially if I’m already stressed, but it doesn’t feel like something taking over my mind exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having intrusive thoughts about the fundraiser that are unwanted and disturbing?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them intrusive exactly. They’re unwanted, sure, and upsetting sometimes, but it feels more like worry that keeps coming back than some disturbing thought I can’t get rid of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when you're not thinking about the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I do feel on edge a lot lately, but I haven’t paid attention to whether it’s there when the fundraiser isn’t on my mind. I guess I’d have to think about that more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious even when you're not thinking about the fundraiser or anything related to it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really separated it out like that. I know I feel tense a lot, but whether it’s there when I’m not thinking about the fundraiser or anything connected to it… I’d have to pay more attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're doing something you enjoy and the fundraiser isn't on your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. If I’m actually caught up in something I enjoy, I don’t think I notice it as much. But I haven’t paid close attention to whether the anxiety is still there when the fundraiser’s completely out of my mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I think I took on way too much with this historical society fundraiser. I was acting like I could run the whole thing myself—starting quilt designs, calling volunteers, making schedules, promising deadlines—and now it’s pretty clear I couldn’t possibly keep up with all of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying about this fundraiser even when there wasn't anything you could do about it at the moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’d think about it when something reminded me, but I don’t know that I was just worrying nonstop when there was nothing I could do. It’s more that the whole thing got bigger than I could handle.\n\nDoctor: Have you been very hard on yourself about not being able to keep up with the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ve been pretty hard on myself. I keep going back over every late order, and every sharp thing I said to the volunteers, and I just think... I let the church down, and the historical society, and really the whole town.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the same thoughts or urges related to the fundraiser, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it repeated urges or anything like that. I do think about it, especially when I’m reminded of something I didn’t finish, but I haven’t really noticed it taking over my mind when I’m trying to focus on something else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about the fundraiser because you want to, or because you can't stop yourself from thinking about it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think it’s exactly because I want to sit and think about it, but I also wouldn’t say it feels like I can’t stop myself. It just comes up when something reminds me, and then I feel bad about it for a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about the fundraiser when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes, but not in a way that feels like it’s stuck in my head. It’ll pop up when I see something related to it, and I wish I wasn’t thinking about it, but then it usually passes after a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts about the fundraiser come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, it can come into my mind when I wasn’t planning to think about it, but usually there’s some reminder. I don’t know if I’d say it just comes out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having thoughts about the fundraiser that you can't control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say I can’t control them. They’re unwanted, sure, and upsetting sometimes, but it feels more like worry that keeps coming back than some disturbing thought I can’t get rid of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when you're not thinking about the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I think I took on way too much with this historical society fundraiser. I was acting like I could run the whole thing myself—starting quilt designs, calling volunteers, making schedules, promising deadlines—and now it’s pretty clear I couldn’t possibly keep up with all of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying about this fundraiser even when there wasn't anything you could do about it at the moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’d think about it when something reminded me, but I don’t know that I was just worrying nonstop when there was nothing I could do. It’s more that the whole thing got bigger than I could handle.\n\nDoctor: Have you been very hard on yourself about not being able to keep up with the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ve been pretty hard on myself. I keep going back over every late order, and every sharp thing I said to the volunteers, and I just think... I let the church down, and the historical society, and really the whole town.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the same thoughts or urges related to the fundraiser, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it repeated urges or anything like that. I do think about it, especially when I’m reminded of something I didn’t finish, but I haven’t really noticed it taking over my mind when I’m trying to focus on something else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about the fundraiser because you want to, or because you can't stop yourself from thinking about it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think it’s exactly because I want to sit and think about it, but I also wouldn’t say it feels like I can’t stop myself. It just comes up when something reminds me, and then I feel bad about it for a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about the fundraiser when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes, but not in a way that feels like it’s stuck in my head. It’ll pop up when I see something related to it, and I wish I wasn’t thinking about it, but then it usually passes after a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts about the fundraiser come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, it can come into my mind when I wasn’t planning to think about it, but usually there’s some reminder. I don’t know if I’d say it just comes out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having thoughts about the fundraiser that you can't control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say I can’t control them. They come up, and I don’t like it when they do, but I can usually shift my mind to something else after a little while.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're thinking about the fundraiser even when you're trying to fall asleep or relax?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been messing with my sleep some. For a while I was only sleeping a few hours and then waking up like I was ready to start sewing or making phone calls. Lately it’s more like I lie there worrying, or I sleep late and still feel worn out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts about the fundraiser are forcing their way into your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it forcing its way in. It pops up sometimes, especially if I’m already stressed, but it doesn’t feel like something taking over my mind exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having intrusive thoughts about the fundraiser that are unwanted and disturbing?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them intrusive exactly. They’re unwanted, sure, and upsetting sometimes, but it feels more like worry that keeps coming back than some disturbing thought I can’t get rid of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when you're not thinking about the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I do feel on edge a lot lately, but I haven’t paid attention to whether it’s there when the fundraiser isn’t on my mind. I guess I’d have to think about that more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious even when you're not thinking about the fundraiser or anything related to it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really separated it out like that. I know I feel tense a lot, but whether it’s there when I’m not thinking about the fundraiser or anything connected to it… I’d have to pay more attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're doing something you enjoy and the fundraiser isn't on your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. If I’m actually caught up in something I enjoy, I don’t think I notice it as much. But I haven’t paid close attention to whether the anxiety is still there when the fundraiser’s completely out of my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're doing something you enjoy and not thinking about the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. When I’m doing something I enjoy, I think I’m more distracted, so I may not notice it as much. I can’t say for sure whether it’s still there or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious even when you're not doing anything and not thinking about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t usually sit and check what I’m feeling when nothing’s going on. I know I can feel tense at times, but whether it happens when I’m not thinking about anything in particular… I haven’t really noticed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s been messing with my sleep some. For a while I was only sleeping a few hours and then waking up like I was ready to start sewing or making phone calls. Lately it’s more like I lie there worrying, or I sleep late and still feel worn out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts about the fundraiser are forcing their way into your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it forcing its way in. It pops up sometimes, especially if I’m already stressed, but it doesn’t feel like something taking over my mind exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having intrusive thoughts about the fundraiser that are unwanted and disturbing?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call them intrusive exactly. They’re unwanted, sure, and upsetting sometimes, but it feels more like worry that keeps coming back than some disturbing thought I can’t get rid of.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when you're not thinking about the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I do feel on edge a lot lately, but I haven’t paid attention to whether it’s there when the fundraiser isn’t on my mind. I guess I’d have to think about that more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious even when you're not thinking about the fundraiser or anything related to it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really separated it out like that. I know I feel tense a lot, but whether it’s there when I’m not thinking about the fundraiser or anything connected to it… I’d have to pay more attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're doing something you enjoy and the fundraiser isn't on your mind?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. If I’m actually caught up in something I enjoy, I don’t think I notice it as much. But I haven’t paid close attention to whether the anxiety is still there when the fundraiser’s completely out of my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're doing something you enjoy and not thinking about the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. When I’m doing something I enjoy, I think I’m more distracted, so I may not notice it as much. I can’t say for sure whether it’s still there or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious even when you're not doing anything and not thinking about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t usually sit and check what I’m feeling when nothing’s going on. I know I can feel tense at times, but whether it happens when I’m not thinking about anything in particular… I haven’t really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not doing anything and not thinking about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t paid enough attention to that exact situation. If I’m just sitting there with nothing going on, I might feel a little uneasy sometimes, but I can’t say for sure it’s anxiety or that it happens when I’m not thinking about anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not doing anything, not thinking about the fundraiser, and not thinking about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. If I’m not doing anything and not thinking about the fundraiser, I don’t really know what I’m feeling in that moment. I haven’t noticed it clearly enough to say yes or no.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
